### Get Command Usage Example (Java)

```java
@NotNull public @NotNull String getUsage()
Gets an example usage of this command 

Returns:
    One or more example usages
```

### Getting Command Examples in Java

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/command/brigadier/argument/CustomArgumentType

The `getExamples` method provides a collection of example string inputs for the argument. These examples are used by the client to help players understand the expected input format, especially when multiple arguments are present in a command node.

```java
default Collection<String> getExamples()
{
    // Return a collection of example strings
    return Arrays.asList("example1", "example2");
}
```

--------------------------------

### Example Command Structure

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

An example demonstrating the hierarchical structure of command definitions, including multiple commands with various properties like description, aliases, permission, permission-message, and usage.

```yaml
commands:
  flagrate:
    description: Set yourself on fire.
    aliases: [combust_me, combustMe]
    permission: inferno.flagrate
    permission-message: You do not have /<permission>
    usage: Syntax error! Perhaps you meant /<command> PlayerName?
  burningdeaths:
    description: List how many times you have died by fire.
    aliases:
    - burning_deaths
    - burningDeaths
    permission: inferno.burningdeaths
    usage: |
      /<command> [player]
      Example: /<command> - see how many times you have burned to death
      Example: /<command> CaptainIce - see how many times CaptainIce has burned to death
  apocalypse:

```

@NotNull public @NotNull Command setUsage(@NotNull @NotNull String usage)
Sets the example usage of this command

    `usage` - new example usage 

Returns:
    this command object, for chaining
```

### Plugin YAML Configuration Example

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

An example of a `plugin.yml` file used to configure a PaperMC plugin. This file specifies essential plugin metadata such as name, version, main class, and description, along with optional fields like provides and authors.

```yaml
name: Inferno
provides: [Hell]
version: 1.4.1
description: This plugin is so 31337. You can set yourself on fire.
# We could place every author in the authors list, but chose not to for illustrative purposes
# Also, having an author distinguishes that person as the project lead, and ensures their
```

### JavaPlugin.init() (Server, Description, DataFolder, File, ClassLoader, Configuration, Logger)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/class-use/Server

Initializes the JavaPlugin with the provided details.

```APIDOC
## POST JavaPlugin.init()

### Description
Initializes the `JavaPlugin` with the provided server, description, data folder, file, class loader, configuration, and logger.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
JavaPlugin.init(Server server, PluginDescriptionFile description, File dataFolder, File file, ClassLoader classLoader, @Nullable PluginMeta configuration, Logger logger)

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **server** (Server) - Required - The server instance.
- **description** (PluginDescriptionFile) - Required - The plugin description file.
- **dataFolder** (File) - Required - The data folder for the plugin.
- **file** (File) - Required - The plugin file.
- **classLoader** (ClassLoader) - Required - The class loader for the plugin.
- **configuration** (PluginMeta) - Optional - The plugin configuration metadata.
- **logger** (Logger) - Required - The logger for the plugin.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "server": "[Server Object]",
  "description": "[PluginDescriptionFile Object]",
  "dataFolder": "[File Object]",
  "file": "[File Object]",
  "classLoader": "[ClassLoader Object]",
  "configuration": "[PluginMeta Object]",
  "logger": "[Logger Object]"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **message** (string) - Indicates successful initialization.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "message": "JavaPlugin initialized successfully."
}
```
```

--------------------------------

  "dataFolder": "[File Object]",
  "file": "[File Object]",

### Creating TypedKey Instances

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/registry/RegistryKey

Demonstrates how to create TypedKey instances for a given registry.

```APIDOC
## TypedKey Creation Methods

### Description
These methods allow you to construct a `TypedKey` for the current registry, identified by a string or a `Key` object.

### Method 1: Using String Key

#### Method
`default TypedKey<T> typedKey(String key)`

#### Description
Constructs a new `TypedKey` for this registry using a string representation of the typed key's key.

#### Parameters
* **key** (String) - Required - The key for the typed key.

### Method 2: Using Key Object

#### Method
`default TypedKey<T> typedKey(Key key)`

#### Description
Constructs a new `TypedKey` for this registry using a `Key` object.

#### Parameters
* **key** (Key) - Required - The `Key` object representing the typed key's key.

### Request Example (Conceptual)
```java
// Assuming 'registryKey' is an instance of RegistryKey<SomeType>
TypedKey<SomeType> myTypedKey = registryKey.typedKey("my_custom_typed_key");
```

### Response Example (Conceptual)
```java
// TypedKey<SomeType> representing the typed key "my_custom_typed_key" for the registry.
```
```

### Bukkit Configuration Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/configuration/package-summary

Provides an overview of the Bukkit configuration classes and their relationships.

```APIDOC
## Bukkit Configuration API

### Description
Classes dedicated to handling a plugin's runtime configuration.

### Related Packages
*   **org.bukkit**: The root package of the Bukkit API.
*   **org.bukkit.configuration.file**: Classes for reading and storing configurations on the filesystem.
*   **org.bukkit.configuration.serialization**: Classes for serialization specialized for Bukkit configuration.

### Key Classes

#### Interfaces
*   **Configuration**: Represents a source of configurable options and settings.
*   **ConfigurationOptions**: Settings for controlling input and output of a `Configuration`.
*   **ConfigurationSection**: Represents a section of a `Configuration`.

#### Classes
*   **InvalidConfigurationException**: Exception thrown when attempting to load an invalid `Configuration`.
*   **MemoryConfiguration**: A `Configuration` implementation that stores all values in memory only.
*   **MemoryConfigurationOptions**: Settings for controlling input and output of a `MemoryConfiguration`.
*   **MemorySection**: A type of `ConfigurationSection` stored in memory.
```

### GET /api/server/buildinfo

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/ServerBuildInfo

Retrieve comprehensive information about the current server build.

```APIDOC
## GET /api/server/buildinfo

### Description
This endpoint retrieves detailed information about the current server build, including version, brand, and Git commit details.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/api/server/buildinfo

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **representation** (ServerBuildInfo.StringRepresentation) - Optional - Specifies the desired string representation format for the build information.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "message": "GET request to retrieve build information"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **minecraftVersionId** (String) - The Minecraft version ID.
- **minecraftVersionName** (String) - The Minecraft version name.
- **brandId** (Key) - The brand ID of the server.
- **brandName** (String) - The brand name of the server.
- **buildNumber** (OptionalInt) - The build number of the server.
- **buildTime** (Instant) - The build time of the server.
- **gitBranch** (Optional<String>) - The Git commit branch.
- **gitCommit** (Optional<String>) - The Git commit hash.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "minecraftVersionId": "1.20.4",
  "minecraftVersionName": "1.20.4",
  "brandId": {
    "id": "papermc:paper"
  },
  "brandName": "Paper",
  "buildNumber": {
    "value": 420
  },
  "buildTime": "2024-01-01T12:00:00Z",
  "gitBranch": {
    "value": "master"
  },
  "gitCommit": {
    "value": "a1b2c3d4e5f6"
  }
}
```
```

### SingleOptionDialogInput Builder and Creation

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/registry/data/dialog/input/class-use/SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry

This section details methods for building and creating `SingleOptionDialogInput` instances using `SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry` objects.

```APIDOC
## SingleOptionDialogInput Construction

### Description
Methods for constructing `SingleOptionDialogInput` instances, including single-option dialogs and their builders, utilizing `SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry`.

### Methods

#### `static SingleOptionDialogInput singleOption(String key, @Range(from=1L, to=1024L) int width, List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry> entries, Component label, boolean labelVisible)`

**Description**: Creates a single-option dialog input, often referred to as a radio input.

**Method**: `static`

**Parameters**:
- `key` (String) - Required - The key identifier for this dialog input.
- `width` (int) - Required - The width of the dialog input (1-1024).
- `entries` (List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry>) - Required - The list of available options for the dialog.
- `label` (Component) - Required - The label for the dialog input.
- `labelVisible` (boolean) - Required - Determines if the label is visible.

#### `static SingleOptionDialogInput.Builder singleOption(String key, Component label, List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry> entries)`

**Description**: Creates a new builder for a single-option dialog input.

**Method**: `static`

**Parameters**:
- `key` (String) - Required - The key identifier for the dialog input.
- `label` (Component) - Required - The label for the dialog input.
- `entries` (List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry>) - Required - The list of options to be included in the dialog.

#### `static SingleOptionDialogInput.Builder singleOptionBuilder(String key, Component label, List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry> entries)`

**Description**: Creates a builder for `SingleOptionDialogInput` with a specified key, label, and a list of option entries. This is an alternative way to construct a single option dialog.

**Method**: `static`

**Parameters**:
- `key` (String) - Required - The unique identifier for the dialog input.
- `label` (Component) - Required - The text label displayed for the dialog input.
- `entries` (List<SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry>) - Required - A list of `SingleOptionDialogInput.OptionEntry` objects representing the choices available to the user.
```

```java
@NotNull public @NotNull List<String> getLoadBeforePlugins()
Description copied from interface: PluginMeta
Provides a list of plugins that should be loaded before this plugin is loaded. The list holds the unique identifiers, following the constraints laid out in `PluginMeta.getName()`, of the plugins that should be loaded before the plugin described by this plugin meta. 
The plugins referenced in the list provided by this method are not considered dependencies of this plugin and are hence not available to the plugin at runtime. They merely load before this plugin. 

Specified by:
    `getLoadBeforePlugins` in interface `PluginMeta` 

Returns:
    immutable list of plugins to load before this plugin

### Get Plugin Load Order (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

Retrieves the phase of server startup during which the plugin should be loaded. Possible values are defined in PluginLoadOrder and default to POSTWORLD. This affects the order in which dependencies are loaded.

```java
public @NotNull PluginLoadOrder getLoad()

Gives the phase of server startup that the plugin should be loaded. 
      * Possible values are in `PluginLoadOrder`. 
      * Defaults to `PluginLoadOrder.POSTWORLD`. 
      * Certain caveats apply to each phase. 
      * When different, `getDepend()`, `getSoftDepend()`, and `getLoadBefore()` become relative in order loaded per-phase. If a plugin loads at `STARTUP`, but a dependency loads at `POSTWORLD`, the dependency will not be loaded before the plugin is loaded. 
In the plugin.yml, this entry is named `load`. 
Example:
> ```
load: STARTUP
```


Returns:
    the phase when the plugin should be loaded
```

### DialogBase Creation with afterAction

## POST /dialogs (Conceptual - Direct Creation)

### Description
This section details how to directly create a `DialogBase` instance, including specifying the action to perform after the dialog closes.

(Conceptual - Static Factory Method)

### Endpoint
(Conceptual - Static Factory Method)

### Parameters
#### Request Body (for Static Factory Method)
- **afterAction** (DialogBase.DialogAfterAction) - Required - The action to take after the dialog is closed.
- **title** (Component) - Required - The title of the dialog.
- **externalTitle** (@Nullable Component) - Optional - An external title for the dialog.
- **canCloseWithEscape** (boolean) - Required - Whether the dialog can be closed with the escape key.
- **pause** (boolean) - Required - Whether the dialog pauses the game.
- **body** (List<? extends DialogBody>) - Required - The content body of the dialog.
- **inputs** (List<? extends DialogInput>) - Required - The input fields for the dialog.

### Request Example (Conceptual Code)

List<DialogBody> body = new ArrayList<>();
List<DialogInput> inputs = new ArrayList<>();

    Component.text("My Dialog"),
    null,
    true,
    false,
    DialogBase.DialogAfterAction.CLOSE_IMMEDIATELY,
    body,
    inputs
);

### Command API Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/command/package-use

This section provides an overview of the core classes within the org.bukkit.command package, focusing on command representation, execution, and sender types.

```APIDOC
## Bukkit Command API

### Description
Provides classes for handling specialized non-chat player input, enabling developers to create and manage in-game commands.

### Core Classes

- **Command**
  - Description: Represents a command that executes various tasks upon user input.
  - Usage: `Command` objects are the fundamental building blocks for defining executable actions within the game.

- **CommandSender**
  - Description: Represents the entity that issues a command. This can be a player, the console, or other server-side entities.
  - Usage: Used to determine the origin of a command and to send feedback or results back to the sender.

- **CommandExecutor**
  - Description: An interface for classes that contain a single method for executing commands. Plugins typically implement this to handle their custom commands.
  - Usage: Implement this interface in your plugin to define the logic for a specific command.

- **TabCompleter**
  - Description: An interface for classes that can suggest tab completions for commands. This enhances user experience by providing command suggestions as players type.
  - Usage: Implement this interface to provide dynamic suggestions for command arguments.

- **PluginCommand**
  - Description: Represents a command that belongs to a specific plugin. These commands are registered and managed by the plugin system.
  - Usage: Used internally by Bukkit to manage commands provided by plugins.

### Related Classes

- **CommandException**
  - Description: Thrown when an unhandled exception occurs during the execution of a Command.

- **CommandMap**
  - Description: Manages the registration and lookup of commands on the server.

- **ConsoleCommandSender**
  - Description: A specific implementation of `CommandSender` representing the server console.

- **TabExecutor**
  - Description: A convenience class that implements both `TabCompleter` and `CommandExecutor`, simplifying the process of creating commands with tab completion.

### Deprecated Classes

- **MessageCommandSender**
  - Description: Deprecated, for removal in a future version. Use `CommandSender` directly.
```

### Skin Model and Setting

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/profile/PlayerTextures

This section details how to get and set the player's skin model and the skin URL itself.

```APIDOC
## Skin Model and Setting

### Methods

#### `getSkinModel()`

*   **Description:** Gets the model of the player's skin.
*   **Method:** N/A (Instance Method)
*   **Return Type:** `@NotNull PlayerTextures.SkinModel`
*   **Returns:** The model of the player's skin. Returns `PlayerTextures.SkinModel.CLASSIC` if no skin is set.

#### `setSkin(@Nullable URL skinUrl)`

*   **Description:** Sets the player's skin to the specified URL and the skin model to `PlayerTextures.SkinModel.CLASSIC`.
*   **Method:** N/A (Instance Method)
*   **Parameters:**
    *   `skinUrl` (URL) - Required - The URL of the player's skin, or `null` to unset it. The URL **must** point to the Minecraft texture server.
*   **Example URL:** `http://textures.minecraft.net/texture/b3fbd454b599df593f57101bfca34e67d292a8861213d2202bb575da7fd091ac`

#### `setSkin(@Nullable URL skinUrl, @Nullable PlayerTextures.SkinModel skinModel)`

*   **Description:** Sets the player's skin and `PlayerTextures.SkinModel`.
*   **Method:** N/A (Instance Method)
*   **Parameters:**
    *   `skinUrl` (URL) - Required - The URL of the player's skin, or `null` to unset it. The URL **must** point to the Minecraft texture server.
    *   `skinModel` (PlayerTextures.SkinModel) - Optional - The skin model. Ignored if the skin URL is `null`. A skin model of `null` results in `PlayerTextures.SkinModel.CLASSIC` to be used.
*   **Example URL:** `http://textures.minecraft.net/texture/b3fbd454b599df593f57101bfca34e67d292a8861213d2202bb575da7fd091ac`
```

### ActionButton Creation and Usage

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/registry/data/dialog/class-use/ActionButton

This section covers the creation of ActionButton instances and their usage in different dialog contexts.

```APIDOC
## ActionButton API

### Description
Provides methods for creating and manipulating `ActionButton` instances, which are interactive elements within dialogs.

### Methods

#### `ActionButton.Builder.build()`

- **Description**: Builds the `ActionButton` instance with the configured values.
- **Method**: `POST` (Conceptual - builder pattern)
- **Endpoint**: N/A
- **Request Body**:
  - `label` (Component) - Required - The text or component to display on the button.
  - `tooltip` (Component) - Optional - The tooltip to show when hovering over the button.
  - `width` (int) - Required - The width of the button (1-1024).
  - `action` (DialogAction) - Optional - The action to perform when the button is clicked.

#### `ActionButton.create()`

- **Description**: Creates a new action button with the specified label, tooltip, width, and action.
- **Method**: `POST` (Conceptual - factory method)
- **Endpoint**: N/A
- **Parameters**:
  - `label` (Component) - Required - The text or component to display on the button.
  - `tooltip` (Component) - Optional - The tooltip to show when hovering over the button.
  - `width` (int) - Required - The width of the button (1-1024).
  - `action` (DialogAction) - Optional - The action to perform when the button is clicked.

### Dialog Integration

`ActionButton` instances are used as parameters in various dialog creation methods and as return types for accessing dialog-specific actions.

#### Example Usage in Dialogs:

- **Confirmation Dialog**: `DialogType.confirmation(ActionButton yesButton, ActionButton noButton)`
- **Notice Dialog**: `DialogType.notice(ActionButton action)`
- **Server Links Dialog**: `DialogType.serverLinks(ActionButton exitAction, int columns, int buttonWidth)`
- **Multi-Action Dialog**: `DialogType.multiAction(List<ActionButton> actions, @Nullable ActionButton exitAction, int columns)`

#### Accessing Action Buttons from Dialog Types:

- **NoticeType**: `action()` returns the `ActionButton`.
- **DialogListType**: `exitAction()` returns the exit `ActionButton` or null.
- **MultiActionType**: `exitAction()` returns the exit `ActionButton` or null.
- **ServerLinksType**: `exitAction()` returns the exit `ActionButton` or null.
- **ConfirmationType**: `noButton()` and `yesButton()` return the respective `ActionButton`s.
- **MultiActionType**: `actions()` returns a list of `ActionButton`s.
```

--------------------------------

### Get Plugin API Provides List (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

Retrieves a list of other plugin APIs that this plugin provides. These are used by other plugins to declare dependencies. The provided APIs must adhere to the same naming conventions as plugin names.

```java
public @NotNull List<String> getProvides()

Gives the list of other plugin APIs which this plugin provides. These are usable for other plugins to depend on. 
      * Must consist of all alphanumeric characters, underscores, hyphon, and period (a-z,A-Z,0-9, _.-). Any other character will cause the plugin.yml to fail loading. 
      * A different plugin providing the same one or using it as their name will not result in the plugin to fail loading. 
      * Case sensitive. 
      * An entry of this list can be referenced in `getDepend()`, `getSoftDepend()`, and `getLoadBefore()`. 
      * `provides` must be in YAML list format. 
In the plugin.yml, this entry is named `provides`. 
Example: 
> ```
provides:
- OtherPluginName
- OldPluginName
```


Returns:
    immutable list of the plugin APIs which this plugin provides
```

--------------------------------

### Get Boots from EntityEquipment (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/EntityEquipment

Retrieves the boots currently worn by an entity. Returns a copy for non-player entities and a live mirror (or null) for player entities. The behavior can be checked by examining the EntityEquipment instance.

```java
EntityEquipment equipment = entity.getEquipment();
if (equipment instanceof PlayerInventory) {
    equipment.getBoots(); // will return a mirror
} else {
    equipment.getBoots(); // will return a copy
}
```

### Get Plugin Name (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

Retrieves the unique name of the plugin, used as an identifier and for determining the data folder. The name must contain only alphanumeric characters, underscores, hyphens, and periods. It is case-sensitive and deprecated with spaces.

```java
public @NotNull String getName()

Gives the name of the plugin. This name is a unique identifier for plugins. 
      * Must consist of all alphanumeric characters, underscores, hyphon, and period (a-z,A-Z,0-9, _.-). Any other character will cause the plugin.yml to fail loading. 
      * Used to determine the name of the plugin's data folder. Data folders are placed in the ./plugins/ directory by default, but this behavior should not be relied on. `Plugin.getDataFolder()` should be used to reference the data folder. 
      * It is good practice to name your jar the same as this, for example 'MyPlugin.jar'. 
      * Case sensitive. 
      * It's the token referenced in `getDepend()`, `getSoftDepend()`, and `getLoadBefore()`. 
      * Using spaces in the plugin's name is deprecated. 
In the plugin.yml, this entry is named `name`. 
Example:
> ```
name: MyPlugin
```


Specified by:
    `getName` in interface `PluginMeta` 

Returns:
    the name of the plugin
```

--------------------------------

### Get Item in Main Hand (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/EntityEquipment

Retrieves the ItemStack currently held in the entity's main hand. Returns a copy for non-player entities or empty stacks, and a live mirror for non-empty stacks from players. Includes example usage to check for mirror behavior.

```java
@NotNull ItemStack getItemInMainHand()
Gets the item the entity is currently holding in their main hand. 
This returns a copy if this equipment instance is from a non-player, or it's an empty stack (has AIR as its type). For non-empty stacks from players, this returns a live mirror. You can check if this will return a mirror with 
```

 EntityEquipment equipment = entity.getEquipment();
 if (equipment instanceof PlayerInventory) {
     equipment.getItemInMainHand(); // will return a mirror
 } else {
     equipment.getItemInMainHand(); // will return a copy
 }
 
```


Returns:
    the currently held item
```

### Get Main Class Name (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

Retrieves the fully qualified name of the plugin's main class. This class is responsible for the plugin's core functionality and should extend JavaPlugin. The format must be resolvable by ClassLoader.loadClass.

```java
public @NotNull String getMain()

Gives the fully qualified name of the main class for a plugin. The format should follow the `ClassLoader.loadClass(String)` syntax to successfully be resolved at runtime. For most plugins, this is the class that extends `JavaPlugin`. 
      * This must contain the full namespace including the class file itself. 
      * If your namespace is `org.bukkit.plugin`, and your class file is called `MyPlugin` then this must be `org.bukkit.plugin.MyPlugin`
      * No plugin can use `org.bukkit.` as a base package for **any class** , including the main class. 
In the plugin.yml, this entry is named `main`. 
Example: 
> ```
main: org.bukkit.plugin.MyPlugin
```


Returns:
    the fully qualified main class for the plugin
```

## Entity EntitySnapshot Methods

### Description

### Get Item in Off Hand (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/EntityEquipment

Retrieves the ItemStack currently held in the entity's off hand. Similar to main hand, it returns a copy for non-player entities or empty stacks, and a live mirror for non-empty stacks from players. Includes example usage to check for mirror behavior.

```java
@NotNull ItemStack getItemInOffHand()
Gets the item the entity is currently holding in their off hand. 
This returns a copy if this equipment instance is from a non-player, or it's an empty stack (has AIR as its type). For non-empty stacks from players, this returns a live mirror. You can check if this will return a mirror with 
```

 EntityEquipment equipment = entity.getEquipment();
 if (equipment instanceof PlayerInventory) {
     equipment.getItemInOffHand(); // will return a mirror
 } else {
     equipment.getItemInOffHand(); // will return a copy
 }
 
```


Returns:
    the currently held item
```

### ProjectileLaunchEvent Details

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/event/entity/ProjectileLaunchEvent

This section details the ProjectileLaunchEvent, including its constructors, methods, and inheritance.

```APIDOC
## ProjectileLaunchEvent API

### Description
This event is called when a projectile is launched in the game. It extends `EntitySpawnEvent` and implements the `Cancellable` interface.

### Constructor
*   **ProjectileLaunchEvent**(`@NotNull Entity` entity)
    *   Creates a new ProjectileLaunchEvent.
    *   Parameters:
        *   `entity` (@NotNull) - The entity involved in the event.

### Methods
*   **@NotNull Projectile** `getEntity()`
    *   Returns the projectile entity involved in this event.
    *   Overrides: `getEntity` in class `EntityEvent`.
*   **boolean** `isCancelled()`
    *   Gets the cancellation state of this event. A cancelled event will not be executed by the server but will still be passed to other plugins.
    *   Specified by: `isCancelled` in interface `Cancellable`.
    *   Overrides: `isCancelled` in class `EntitySpawnEvent`.
    *   Returns: `true` if this event is cancelled.
*   **void** `setCancelled(boolean` cancel`)
    *   Sets the cancellation state of this event. A cancelled event will not be executed by the server but will still be passed to other plugins.
    *   Specified by: `setCancelled` in interface `Cancellable`.
    *   Overrides: `setCancelled` in class `EntitySpawnEvent`.
    *   Parameters:
        *   `cancel` - `true` if you wish to cancel this event.

### Inherited Methods
*   **From `EntitySpawnEvent`**: `getHandlerList`, `getHandlers`, `getLocation`
*   **From `EntityEvent`**: `getEntityType`
*   **From `Event`**: `callEvent`, `getEventName`, `isAsynchronous`
*   **From `Object`**: `clone`, `equals`, `finalize`, `getClass`, `hashCode`, `notify`, `notifyAll`, `toString`, `wait`, `wait`, `wait`

### Example Usage (Conceptual)
```java
import org.bukkit.event.EventHandler;
import org.bukkit.event.Listener;
import org.bukkit.event.entity.ProjectileLaunchEvent;

public class ProjectileListener implements Listener {

    @EventHandler
    public void onProjectileLaunch(ProjectileLaunchEvent event) {
        // Get the projectile entity
        Projectile projectile = event.getEntity();

        // Check if the projectile is a ThrowableProjectile (e.g., snowball, egg)
        if (projectile instanceof org.bukkit.entity.ThrowableProjectile) {
            // Optionally cancel the event
            // event.setCancelled(true);
        }
    }
}
```
```

public @NotNull List<String> getAuthors()

Gives the list of authors for the plugin. 
      * Gives credit to the developer.

      * `authors` must be in YAML list format.

### Get Viewers

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/Inventory

Gets a list of players viewing the inventory.

```APIDOC
## GET /inventory/viewers

### Description
Gets a list of players viewing the inventory. Note that a player is considered to be viewing their own inventory and internal crafting screen even when said inventory is not open. They will normally be considered to be viewing their inventory even when they have a different inventory screen open, but it's possible for customized inventory screens to exclude the viewer's inventory, so this should never be assumed to be non-empty.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/inventory/viewers

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **viewers** (List<HumanEntity>) - A list of HumanEntities who are viewing this Inventory.

#### Response Example
{
  "viewers": [
    "Player1",
    "Player2"
  ]
}
```

### Getting and Setting Teleport Destination (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/event/entity/EntityPortalEvent

The `getTo()` method retrieves the destination location for the teleportation event. For Nether portals, this is the starting point for portal searching, while for Ender portals, it's the exact destination. The `setTo()` method allows modification of this destination, including canceling the teleportation by setting it to null.

```java
@Nullable public @Nullable Location getTo()

```

```java
public void setTo(@Nullable @Nullable Location to)

```

### Define Plugin Awareness

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginDescriptionFile

The `awareness` field in `plugin.yml` specifies a list of `PluginAwareness` flags that a plugin developer acknowledges. Each awareness starts with `!@`. Unrecognized or future awareness entries are gracefully replaced by a generic object. Previous Bukkit versions may fail to load plugins with awareness defined.

```yaml
awareness:
- !@UTF8
```

### ConfigurationSection Interface Methods

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/configuration/class-use/ConfigurationSection

Details methods for creating, retrieving, and managing configuration sections.

```APIDOC
## ConfigurationSection Methods

### Description
Methods related to creating and retrieving `ConfigurationSection` objects.

### Methods

#### `createSection(@NotNull String path)`
* **Method**: POST
* **Endpoint**: N/A (Interface method)
* **Description**: Creates an empty `ConfigurationSection` at the specified path.
* **Return Type**: `@NotNull ConfigurationSection`

#### `createSection(@NotNull String path, @NotNull Map<?,?> map)`
* **Method**: POST
* **Endpoint**: N/A (Interface method)
* **Description**: Creates a `ConfigurationSection` at the specified path, with specified values.
* **Return Type**: `@NotNull ConfigurationSection`

#### `getConfigurationSection(@NotNull String path)`
* **Method**: GET
* **Endpoint**: N/A (Interface method)
* **Description**: Gets the requested `ConfigurationSection` by path.
* **Return Type**: `@Nullable ConfigurationSection`

#### `getDefaultSection()`
* **Method**: GET
* **Endpoint**: N/A (Interface method)
* **Description**: Gets the equivalent `ConfigurationSection` from the default `Configuration` defined in `getRoot()`.
* **Return Type**: `@Nullable ConfigurationSection`

#### `getParent()`
* **Method**: GET
* **Endpoint**: N/A (Interface method)
* **Description**: Gets the parent `ConfigurationSection` that directly contains this `ConfigurationSection`.
* **Return Type**: `@Nullable ConfigurationSection`
```

### FileConfiguration - loadFromString

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/configuration/file/FileConfiguration

Loads a FileConfiguration from a given string. Clears existing values and applies new ones from the string. Throws an exception if the string is invalid or null.

```APIDOC
## POST /websites/jd_papermc_io_paper_1_21_11/loadFromString

### Description
Loads this `FileConfiguration` from the specified string, as opposed to from file. All the values contained within this configuration will be removed, leaving only settings and defaults, and the new values will be loaded from the given string. If the string is invalid in any way, an exception will be thrown.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/websites/jd_papermc_io_paper_1_21_11/loadFromString

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **contents** (String) - Required - Contents of a Configuration to load.

### Request Example
{
  "contents": "example configuration string"
}

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **status** (string) - Indicates success.

#### Response Example
{
  "status": "loaded"
}

### Errors
- `InvalidConfigurationException` - Thrown if the specified string is invalid.
- `IllegalArgumentException` - Thrown if contents is null.
```

```

## getDispatcher

Gets the underlying `CommandDispatcher`. This is a delicate API that must be used with care to ensure a consistent user experience. It is preferred to use `register methods` over directly registering to the dispatcher.

### Method
`@Experimental com.mojang.brigadier.CommandDispatcher<CommandSourceStack> getDispatcher()`

### Parameters
None

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **dispatcher** (CommandDispatcher) - The dispatcher instance.

}

### PlayerPostRespawnEvent Documentation

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/com/destroystokyo/paper/event/player/PlayerPostRespawnEvent

Detailed documentation for the PlayerPostRespawnEvent, including its inheritance, fields, constructors, and methods.

```APIDOC
## PlayerPostRespawnEvent

### Description
Fired after a player has respawned.

### Class Hierarchy
`java.lang.Object` -> `org.bukkit.event.Event` -> `org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerEvent` -> `io.papermc.paper.event.player.AbstractRespawnEvent` -> `com.destroystokyo.paper.event.player.PlayerPostRespawnEvent`

### Constructor Summary
* `PlayerPostRespawnEvent(Player respawnPlayer, Location respawnLocation, boolean isBedSpawn, boolean isAnchorSpawn, boolean missingRespawnBlock, PlayerRespawnEvent.RespawnReason respawnReason)`: Constructs a new PlayerPostRespawnEvent.

### Field Summary
* `player` (Player): Inherited from `PlayerEvent`.
* `respawnLocation` (Location): Inherited from `AbstractRespawnEvent`.

### Method Summary
* `getRespawnedLocation()` (Location): Returns the location of the respawned player.
* `getHandlers()` (HandlerList): Gets the handler list for this event.
* `getHandlerList()` (HandlerList): Gets the handler list for this event.

### Constructor Details
* **PlayerPostRespawnEvent**
  ```java
  @Internal
  public PlayerPostRespawnEvent(Player respawnPlayer, Location respawnLocation, boolean isBedSpawn, boolean isAnchorSpawn, boolean missingRespawnBlock, PlayerRespawnEvent.RespawnReason respawnReason)
  ```

### Method Details
* **getRespawnedLocation**
  ```java
  @Obsolete
  public Location getRespawnedLocation()
  ```
  Returns the location of the respawned player.
  Returns: location of the respawned player

* **getHandlers**
  ```java
  public HandlerList getHandlers()
  ```
  Specified by: `getHandlers` in class `Event`

* **getHandlerList**
  ```java
  public static HandlerList getHandlerList()
  ```
```

### Server API: Inventory Creation with InventoryHolder

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/class-use/InventoryHolder

Documentation for methods within the Server class that create inventories and accept an InventoryHolder.

```APIDOC
## Server API: Inventory Creation with InventoryHolder

### Description
Provides methods to create various types of inventories on the server, optionally associating them with an `InventoryHolder`.

### Methods

#### `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, int size)`

- **Description**: Creates an empty inventory of type `InventoryType.CHEST` with the specified size.
- **Method**: `@NotNull Inventory`
- **Parameters**:
  - `owner` (InventoryHolder) - Optional - The holder of the inventory.
  - `size` (int) - Required - The size of the inventory.

#### `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, int size, @NotNull Component title)`

- **Description**: Creates an empty inventory of type `InventoryType.CHEST` with the specified size and title.
- **Method**: `@NotNull Inventory`
- **Parameters**:
  - `owner` (InventoryHolder) - Optional - The holder of the inventory.
  - `size` (int) - Required - The size of the inventory.
  - `title` (Component) - Required - The title of the inventory.

#### `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, @NotNull InventoryType type)`

- **Description**: Creates an empty inventory with the specified type.
- **Method**: `@NotNull Inventory`
- **Parameters**:
  - `owner` (InventoryHolder) - Optional - The holder of the inventory.
  - `type` (InventoryType) - Required - The type of the inventory.

#### `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, @NotNull InventoryType type, @NotNull Component title)`

- **Description**: Creates an empty inventory with the specified type and title.
- **Method**: `@NotNull Inventory`
- **Parameters**:
  - `owner` (InventoryHolder) - Optional - The holder of the inventory.
  - `type` (InventoryType) - Required - The type of the inventory.
  - `title` (Component) - Required - The title of the inventory.

### Deprecated Methods

- `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, int size, @NotNull String title)`: Deprecated in favour of `Server.createInventory(InventoryHolder, int, net.kyori.adventure.text.Component)`.
- `createInventory(@Nullable InventoryHolder owner, @NotNull InventoryType type, @NotNull String title)`: Deprecated in favour of `Server.createInventory(InventoryHolder, InventoryType, net.kyori.adventure.text.Component)`.
```

### MemoryConfiguration - Methods

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/configuration/MemoryConfiguration

Documented methods for interacting with MemoryConfiguration, including setting defaults, retrieving values, and managing options.

```APIDOC
## MemoryConfiguration Methods

### Description
Methods for managing default values, options, and retrieving configuration data from a `MemoryConfiguration` object.

### Method
`void addDefault(@NotNull String path, @Nullable Object value)`

**Description:** Sets the default value in the root at the given path as provided.

**Parameters:**
- `path` (String) - Required - The path to the default value.
- `value` (Object) - Optional - The default value to set.

### Method
`void addDefaults(@NotNull Map<String, Object> defaults)`

**Description:** Sets the default values of the given paths as provided.

**Parameters:**
- `defaults` (Map<String, Object>) - Required - A map of paths and their default values.

### Method
`void addDefaults(@NotNull Configuration defaults)`

**Description:** Sets the default values of the given paths as provided.

**Parameters:**
- `defaults` (Configuration) - Required - A `Configuration` object containing default values.

### Method
`@Nullable Configuration getDefaults()`

**Description:** Gets the source `Configuration` for this configuration.

**Returns:**
- `@Nullable Configuration` - The source `Configuration` for defaults.

### Method
`@Nullable ConfigurationSection getParent()`

**Description:** Gets the parent `ConfigurationSection` that directly contains this `ConfigurationSection`.

**Returns:**
- `@Nullable ConfigurationSection` - The parent `ConfigurationSection`.

### Method
`@NotNull MemoryConfigurationOptions options()`

**Description:** Gets the `ConfigurationOptions` for this `Configuration`.

**Returns:**
- `@NotNull MemoryConfigurationOptions` - The `ConfigurationOptions`.

### Method
`void setDefaults(@NotNull Configuration defaults)`

**Description:** Sets the source of all default values for this `Configuration`.

**Parameters:**
- `defaults` (Configuration) - Required - The `Configuration` to use as the source for default values.
```

### Get Inventory Location

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/inventory/Inventory

Get the location of the block or entity which corresponds to this inventory.

```APIDOC
## GET /inventory/location

### Description
Get the location of the block or entity which corresponds to this inventory. May return null if this container was custom created or is a virtual / subcontainer.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/inventory/location

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **location** (Location) - The location of the inventory holder, or null if not applicable.

#### Response Example
{
  "location": {
    "world": "world_name",
    "x": 100.5,
    "y": 64.0,
    "z": -200.25
  }
}
```

### ProviderUtil - Class Loading

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/plugin/provider/util/ProviderUtil

This section covers the methods used to load a class by its fully qualified name, instantiate it, and cast it to a specified parent type using a given classloader.

```APIDOC
## GET /api/providerutil/loadClass

### Description
Loads a class by its fully qualified name from a given classloader, instantiates it using its no-args constructor, and casts it to a specified parent type.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/api/providerutil/loadClass

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **clazz** (String) - Required - The fully qualified name of the class to load.
- **classType** (Class<T>) - Required - The parent type to which the instantiated object should be cast.
- **loader** (ClassLoader) - Required - The classloader from which the class should be loaded.
- **onError** (Runnable) - Optional - A runnable that is executed before any unknown exception is raised.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "clazz": "com.example.MyProvider",
  "classType": "JavaPlugin",
  "loader": "org.bukkit.plugin.java.PluginClassLoader",
  "onError": "() -> System.out.println('Error loading class')"
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **T** (Object) - The instantiated object from the loaded class, cast to the parent type.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "instance": "com.example.MyProvider@12345"
}
```
```

None

Gets the type of this rule, which can be an Integer or Boolean.

### Method
`@NotNull public abstract @NotNull Class<T> getType()`

### Parameters
None

### Returns
`@NotNull Class<T>` - the rule type (Integer or Boolean).
```

```APIDOC
## Method: getByName

### Description
Gets a `GameRule` by its name. This method is deprecated and will be removed in a future version. Game rules are now managed by a registry, and elements should be fetched from it.

### Method
`@Nullable @Deprecated(since="1.21.11", forRemoval=true) public static <T> @Nullable GameRule<T> getByName(@NotNull @NotNull String rule)`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
- **rule** (`@NotNull String`) - Required - The name of the GameRule to retrieve.

### Returns
`@Nullable GameRule<T>` - The `GameRule` instance if found, otherwise null.
```

```APIDOC
## Method: values

### Description
Gets an immutable collection of all registered `GameRule`s. This method is deprecated and will be removed in a future version. Elements should be fetched from the GameRule registry instead.

### Method
`@NotNull @Deprecated(since="1.21.11", forRemoval=true) public static @NotNull GameRule<?>[] values()`

### Parameters
None

### Returns
`@NotNull GameRule<?>[]` - An immutable array containing all registered GameRules.
```

## Method: translationKey

### Description

### Method
`@NotNull public abstract @NotNull String translationKey()`

None

### Returns
`@NotNull String` - The translation key.

### Java Server Tick Start Event getTickNumber Method

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/com/destroystokyo/paper/event/server/ServerTickStartEvent

This method returns the current server tick number. The tick count starts from 1 for the very first tick after the server starts. This is useful for tracking game progress or scheduling actions.

```java
/**
 * Returns the current tick number since the server started.
 * The first tick is numbered 1.
 *
 * @return The current tick number.
 */
public int getTickNumber()
```

### Bukkit API Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/package-summary

This section provides an overview of the classes and interfaces available in the root Bukkit API package.

```APIDOC
## Bukkit API Package

### Description
The root package of the Bukkit API, containing generalized API classes.

### Classes and Interfaces

*   **Art**: Represents the art on a painting.
*   **Axis**: Represents a mutually perpendicular axis in 3D Cartesian coordinates.
*   **BanEntry<T>**: A single entry from a ban list.
*   **BanList<T>**: A ban list, containing bans of some `BanList.Type`.
*   **BanList.Type**: Deprecated. Use `BanListType`.
*   **BlockChangeDelegate**: A delegate for handling block changes.
*   **Bukkit**: Represents the Bukkit core, for version and Server singleton handling.
*   **ChatColor**: Deprecated. Use Adventure API.
*   **Chunk**: Represents a chunk of blocks.
*   **Chunk.LoadLevel**: An enum to specify the load level of a chunk.
*   **ChunkSnapshot**: Represents a static, thread-safe snapshot of chunk of blocks.
*   **CoalType**: Deprecated, for removal.
*   **Color**: A container for a color palette.
*   **CropState**: Deprecated, for removal. Use `BlockData`.
*   **Difficulty**: Represents the various difficulty levels.
*   **DyeColor**: All supported color values for dyes and cloth.
*   **Effect**: A list of effects that the server is able to send to players.
*   **Effect.Type**: Deprecated.
*   **EntityEffect**: A list of all effects that can happen to entities.
*   **ExplosionResult**: Represents the outcome of an explosion.
*   **FeatureFlag**: Represents a Feature Flag for a `FeatureFlagSetHolder`.
*   **FireworkEffect**: Represents a single firework effect.
*   **FireworkEffect.Builder**: A builder for FireworkEffects.
*   **FireworkEffect.Type**: The type or shape of the effect.
*   **Fluid**: Represents a fluid type.
*   **FluidCollisionMode**: Determines collision behavior when fluids get hit during ray tracing.
*   **GameEvent**: Represents a generic Mojang game event.
*   **GameMode**: Represents the various game modes.
*   **GameRule<T>**: Dictates certain behavior within Minecraft.
*   **GameRules**: All the vanilla gamerules.
*   **GrassSpecies**: Deprecated, for removal. Use `BlockData`.
*   **HeightMap**: Information regarding heightmaps.
*   **Input**: Represents a movement input applied to an entity.
*   **Instrument**: [Description needed]
*   **JukeboxSong**: Represents a song which may play in a Jukebox.
*   **Keyed**: Represents an object which has a `NamespacedKey` attached to it.
*   **Location**: Represents a 3-dimensional position in a world.
*   **Material**: An enum of all material IDs accepted by the server and client.
*   **MinecraftExperimental**: Indicates an element is part of a Minecraft experimental feature.
*   **MinecraftExperimental.Requires**: An enum identifying a feature flag required by a `MinecraftExperimental` feature.
*   **MusicInstrument**: [Description needed]
*   **Nameable**: Represents a block, entity, or other object that may receive a custom name.
*   **NamespacedKey**: Represents a String based key with a namespace and a key.
*   **NetherWartsState**: Deprecated, for removal. Use `BlockData` and `Ageable`.
*   **Note**: A note class to store a specific note.
*   **Note.Tone**: An enum holding tones.
*   **OfflinePlayer**: Represents a reference to a player identity and stored data.
*   **Particle**: [Description needed]
*   **Particle.DustOptions**: Options for dust particles.
*   **Particle.DustTransition**: Options for color transitioning dust particles.
*   **Particle.Spell**: [Description needed]
*   **Particle.Trail**: Options for trail particles.
*   **PortalType**: Represents various types of portals.
*   **Raid**: Represents a raid event.
*   **Raid.RaidStatus**: Represents the status of a `Raid`.
*   **RegionAccessor**: Provides access to getting, modifying, and spawning Biome, BlockState, and Entity.
*   **Registry<T extends Keyed>**: Represents a registry of Bukkit objects retrievable by `NamespacedKey`.
*   **Rotation**: An enum for rotation based orientation.
*   **SandstoneType**: Deprecated, for removal. Use `BlockData`.
*   **Server**: Represents a server implementation.
*   **Server.Spigot**: [Description needed]
*   **ServerLinks**: Represents collections of links sent to a client.
*   **ServerLinks.ServerLink**: Represents a server link.
*   **ServerLinks.Type**: Represents a known link type.
*   **ServerTickManager**: Manages ticking within a server.
*   **SkullType**: Deprecated, for removal.
```

### Constructing a Note Instance (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/Note

Demonstrates how to create a `Note` object using its constructors. The first constructor takes an integer representing the internal note ID, while the second allows specifying the octave, tone, and whether the note is sharped.

```java
public Note(int note)
Creates a new note.
Parameters:
    `note` - Internal note id. `getId()` always return this value. The value has to be in the interval [0; 24].
```

```java
public Note(int octave, @NotNull @NotNull Note.Tone tone, boolean sharped)
Creates a new note.
Parameters:
    `octave` - The octave where the note is in. Has to be 0 - 2.     `tone` - The tone within the octave. If the octave is 2 the note has to be F#.     `sharped` - Set if the tone is sharped (e.g. for F#).
```

--------------------------------

### Creating Specific Note Tones (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/Note

Provides examples of static methods used to create `Note` objects with specific tonal qualities: sharp, flat, or natural. These methods simplify the process of instantiating notes with predefined characteristics.

```java
@NotNull public static @NotNull Note sharp(int octave, @NotNull @NotNull Note.Tone tone)
Creates a new note for a sharp tone, such as A-sharp.
Parameters:
    `octave` - The octave where the note is in. Has to be 0 - 2.     `tone` - The tone within the octave. If the octave is 2 the note has to be F#.
Returns:
    The new note.
```

```java
@NotNull public static @NotNull Note flat(int octave, @NotNull @NotNull Note.Tone tone)
Creates a new note for a flat tone, such as A-flat.
Parameters:
    `octave` - The octave where the note is in. Has to be 0 - 1.     `tone` - The tone within the octave.
Returns:
    The new note.
```

```java
@NotNull public static @NotNull Note natural(int octave, @NotNull @NotNull Note.Tone tone)
Creates a new note for a natural tone, such as A-natural.
Parameters:
    `octave` - The octave where the note is in. Has to be 0 - 1.     `tone` - The tone within the octave.
Returns:
    The new note.
```

### Bukkit API Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/package-use

This section provides an overview of key classes and enums within the Bukkit API, including their descriptions and any deprecation notices.

```APIDOC
## Bukkit API Classes

### TreeSpecies

**Description:** Represents tree species.

**Deprecated:** This API element is subject to removal in a future version.

### WeatherType

**Description:** An enum of all current weather types.

### World

**Description:** Represents a world, which may contain entities, chunks, and blocks.

### WorldBorder

**Description:** Represents the world border.

### Nameable

**Description:** Represents a block, entity, or other object that may receive a custom name.

### Keyed

**Description:** Represents an object which has a `NamespacedKey` attached to it.

### Location

**Description:** Represents a 3-dimensional position in a world.

### NamespacedKey

**Description:** Represents a String based key which consists of two components - a namespace and a key.

### ExplosionResult

**Description:** Represents the outcome of an explosion.

### GameEvent

**Description:** Represents a generic Mojang game event.

### Instrument

**Description:** Represents musical instruments.

### Material

**Description:** An enum of all material IDs accepted by the official server and client.

### Note

**Description:** A note class to store a specific note.

### DyeColor

**Description:** All supported color values for dyes and cloth.

### PortalType

**Description:** Represents various types of portals that can be made in a world.

### Sound

**Description:** An interface of Sounds the server is able to send to players.

### SoundCategory

**Description:** An Enum of categories for sounds.

### Warning

**Description:** This designates the warning state for a specific item.

### GameMode

**Description:** Represents the various types of game modes that `HumanEntity`s may have.

### Input

**Description:** Represents a movement input applied to an entity.

### ServerLinks

**Description:** Represents a collection of links which may be sent to a client.

### Statistic

**Description:** Represents a countable statistic, which is tracked by the server.

### Raid

**Description:** Represents a raid event.

### UndefinedNullability

**Description:** Deprecated. This should generally not be used in any new API code as it suggests a bad API design.

### Chunk

**Description:** Represents a chunk of blocks.

### HeightMap

**Description:** Further information regarding heightmaps.

### RegionAccessor

**Description:** A RegionAccessor gives access to getting, modifying and spawning `Biome`, `BlockState` and `Entity`, as well as generating some basic structures.

### World.Environment

**Description:** Represents various map environment types that a world may be.

### TreeType

**Description:** Tree and organic structure types.
```

### BooleanDialogInput Builder

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/registry/data/dialog/input/class-use/BooleanDialogInput

Illustrates how to build a BooleanDialogInput instance using its Builder pattern.

```APIDOC
## POST /api/dialog/input/boolean/build

### Description
Builds a BooleanDialogInput instance from its builder configuration.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/api/dialog/input/boolean/build

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **builderConfig** (Object) - Required - Configuration for the BooleanDialogInput builder.
  - **key** (String) - Required - The key for the dialog input.
  - **label** (Component) - Required - The label for the dialog input.
  - **initialValue** (boolean) - Required - The initial value of the boolean input.
  - **valueIfTrue** (String) - Required - The value when the input is true.
  - **valueIfFalse** (String) - Required - The value when the input is false.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "builderConfig": {
    "key": "setting_key",
    "label": {"text": "Dark Mode"},
    "initialValue": false,
    "valueIfTrue": "dark_mode_on",
    "valueIfFalse": "dark_mode_off"
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **dialogInput** (BooleanDialogInput) - The built boolean dialog input object.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "dialogInput": {
    "type": "BOOLEAN",
    "key": "setting_key",
    "label": {"text": "Dark Mode"},
    "initialValue": false,
    "valueIfTrue": "dark_mode_on",
    "valueIfFalse": "dark_mode_off"
  }
}
```
```

### Server Properties and Configuration

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/Bukkit

Retrieve various configuration settings and properties of the server.

```APIDOC
## GET /server/properties

### Description
Retrieves a list of command aliases, connection throttle, generate-structures setting, hide-online-players status, idle timeout, IP address, banned IPs, max chained neighbor updates, max players, max world size, online mode status, and pause-when-empty time.

### Method
GET

### Endpoint
/server/properties

### Parameters
None

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **commandAliases** (List<String>) - A list of command aliases defined in the server properties.
- **connectionThrottle** (long) - The value of the connection throttle setting.
- **generateStructures** (boolean) - The state of the generate-structures setting.
- **hideOnlinePlayers** (boolean) - Whether the server hides online players in server status.
- **idleTimeout** (int) - The idle kick timeout in seconds.
- **ip** (String) - The IP address the server is bound to, or an empty string if not specified.
- **ipBans** (Set<String>) - A set containing all currently banned IP addresses.
- **maxChainedNeighborUpdates** (int) - The maximum number of consecutive neighbor updates before skipping additional ones.
- **maxPlayers** (int) - The maximum number of players allowed to log in to the server.
- **maxWorldSize** (int) - The maximum world size.
- **onlineMode** (boolean) - Whether the server is in online mode.
- **pauseWhenEmptyTime** (int) - The pause-when-empty threshold in seconds.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "commandAliases": ["list", "alias"],
  "connectionThrottle": 1000,
  "generateStructures": true,
  "hideOnlinePlayers": false,
  "idleTimeout": 300,
  "ip": "0.0.0.0",
  "ipBans": ["192.168.1.1"],
  "maxChainedNeighborUpdates": 10,
  "maxPlayers": 20,
  "maxWorldSize": 10000000,
  "onlineMode": true,
  "pauseWhenEmptyTime": 60
}
```
```

### org.bukkit.plugin Package Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/package-use

This section details the primary classes within the `org.bukkit.plugin` package, which are fundamental for loading and managing plugins in a Bukkit-compatible environment.

```APIDOC
## Package: org.bukkit.plugin

### Description
Classes specifically relating to loading software modules at runtime.

### Key Classes:

- **EventExecutor** (Interface) - Interface which defines the class for event call backs to plugins.
- **InvalidDescriptionException** (Exception) - Thrown when attempting to load an invalid PluginDescriptionFile.
- **InvalidPluginException** (Exception) - Thrown when attempting to load an invalid Plugin file.
- **Plugin** (Interface) - Represents a Plugin.
- **PluginAwareness** (Class) - Represents a concept that a plugin is aware of.
- **PluginAwareness.Flags** (Class) - Each entry here represents a particular plugin's awareness.
- **PluginDescriptionFile** (Class) - This type is the runtime-container for the information in the plugin.yml.
- **PluginLoader** (Interface) - Deprecated, for removal: This API element is subject to removal in a future version.
- **PluginLoadOrder** (Enum) - Represents the order in which a plugin should be initialized and enabled.
- **PluginManager** (Interface) - Handles all plugin management from the Server.
- **RegisteredListener** (Class) - Stores relevant information for plugin listeners.
- **RegisteredServiceProvider** (Interface) - A registered service provider.
- **ServicePriority** (Enum) - Represents various priorities of a provider.
- **ServicesManager** (Interface) - Manages services and service providers.
- **UnknownDependencyException** (Exception) - Exception thrown when a plugin's dependency is not met.
```

### io.papermc.paper.registry.keys.PaintingVariantKeys.create

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/class-use/Art

Creates a typed key for Art in the minecraft:painting_variant registry.

```APIDOC
## PaintingVariantKeys.create(Key key)

### Description
Creates a typed key for `Art` in the `minecraft:painting_variant` registry, allowing for custom painting variants to be registered or accessed.

### Method
`POST` (Implied - Creating a key)

### Endpoint
N/A (Static method)

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **key** (Key) - Required - The namespaced key for the painting variant.

### Request Example
```java
// Assuming 'myCustomKey' is a valid NamespacedKey
Key myCustomKey = NamespacedKey.minecraft("my_custom_art");
TypedKey<Art> customArtKey = PaintingVariantKeys.create(myCustomKey);
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **TypedKey<Art>** - The created typed key for the Art.

#### Response Example
```json
// Represents the created key (not directly returned as JSON)
{
  "key": "minecraft:my_custom_art",
  "type": "Art"
}
```
```

### SmoothBrick Class Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/material/SmoothBrick

Provides an overview of the SmoothBrick class, its inheritance, and deprecation warnings.

```APIDOC
## SmoothBrick Class

### Description
Represents the different types of smooth bricks. This class is deprecated and subject to removal in future versions. All usage of `MaterialData` is deprecated; use `BlockData` instead.

### Inheritance
- `java.lang.Object`
  - `org.bukkit.material.MaterialData`
    - `org.bukkit.material.TexturedMaterial`
      - `org.bukkit.material.SmoothBrick`

### Deprecation Status
- `@Deprecated(since="1.13", forRemoval=true)` - The class itself is deprecated.
- All methods and constructors are also deprecated.
```

### MemoryConfiguration Overview

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/configuration/class-use/MemoryConfiguration

Provides information on the MemoryConfiguration class, its constructors, and methods that return or use it.

```APIDOC
## MemoryConfiguration Usage in org.bukkit.configuration

### Description
This section outlines how `MemoryConfiguration` is utilized within the `org.bukkit.configuration` package, including methods that return `MemoryConfiguration` instances and constructors that accept it as a parameter.

### Methods Returning MemoryConfiguration

- **`MemoryConfigurationOptions.configuration()`**
  - **Description**: Returns a `MemoryConfiguration` instance.
  - **Returns**: `@NotNull MemoryConfiguration`

### Constructors Accepting MemoryConfiguration

- **`MemoryConfigurationOptions(MemoryConfiguration configuration)`**
  - **Description**: Constructs `MemoryConfigurationOptions` with a given `MemoryConfiguration`.
  - **Parameters**:
    - `configuration` (MemoryConfiguration) - Required - The MemoryConfiguration to associate with these options.
  - **Modifier**: `protected`

## MemoryConfiguration Usage in org.bukkit.configuration.file

### Description
This section details the subclasses of `MemoryConfiguration` found in the `org.bukkit.configuration.file` package and constructors that accept `MemoryConfiguration`.

### Subclasses of MemoryConfiguration

- **`FileConfiguration`**
  - **Description**: A base class for file-based `Configuration` implementations.
  - **Modifier**: `class`

- **`YamlConfiguration`**
  - **Description**: An implementation of `Configuration` that saves files in Yaml format.
  - **Modifier**: `class`

### Constructors Accepting MemoryConfiguration

- **`FileConfigurationOptions(MemoryConfiguration configuration)`**
  - **Description**: Constructs `FileConfigurationOptions` with a given `MemoryConfiguration`.
  - **Parameters**:
    - `configuration` (MemoryConfiguration) - Required - The MemoryConfiguration to associate with these options.
  - **Modifier**: `protected`
```

### BundleContents.bundleContents()

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/datacomponent/item/class-use/BundleContents

Creating a BundleContents object from a list of ItemStacks.

```APIDOC
## BundleContents.bundleContents(List<ItemStack> contents)

### Description
This static method allows you to create a new `BundleContents` object, which encapsulates a list of `ItemStack` objects. This is useful for setting the contents of a bundle DataComponent.

### Method
`static BundleContents`

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **contents** (List<ItemStack>) - Required - A list of `ItemStack` objects to be included in the bundle.

### Request Example
```java
List<ItemStack> itemsInBundle = new ArrayList<>();
itemsInBundle.add(new ItemStack(Items.DIAMOND, 1));
itemsInBundle.add(new ItemStack(Items.IRON_INGOT, 5));

BundleContents newBundleContents = BundleContents.bundleContents(itemsInBundle);

// Now you can set this BundleContents to an item's DataComponent
// itemStack.set(DataComponentTypes.BUNDLE_CONTENTS, newBundleContents);
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
Returns a `BundleContents` object representing the provided list of items.

#### Response Example
```json
// This is a conceptual representation of the returned BundleContents object
{
  "items": [
    {
      "type": "minecraft:diamond",
      "count": 1
    },
    {
      "type": "minecraft:iron_ingot",
      "count": 5
    }
  ]
}
```
```

### ItemMeta and NamespacedKey

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/class-use/NamespacedKey

Demonstrates methods within org.bukkit.inventory.meta that interact with NamespacedKey for item properties like tooltips, recipes, and item models.

```APIDOC
## ItemMeta and NamespacedKey

### Description
Methods for interacting with item metadata that utilize NamespacedKey for custom properties, recipes, and item models.

### Methods:

- **`getTooltipStyle()`** (ItemMeta): Returns the custom tooltip style associated with the item, represented by a `NamespacedKey`.
- **`getRecipes()`** (KnowledgeBookMeta): Retrieves a list of `NamespacedKey` representing all recipes contained within a Knowledge Book.
- **`addRecipe(@NotNull NamespacedKey... recipes)`** (KnowledgeBookMeta): Adds one or more recipes, identified by `NamespacedKey`, to the Knowledge Book.
- **`setItemModel(@Nullable NamespacedKey itemModel)`** (ItemMeta): Sets the custom item model for the item using a `NamespacedKey`.
- **`setNoteBlockSound(@Nullable NamespacedKey noteBlockSound)`** (SkullMeta): Sets the `NamespacedKey` for the sound to be played when the skull is placed on a note block.
- **`setTooltipStyle(@Nullable NamespacedKey tooltipStyle)`** (ItemMeta): Sets the custom tooltip style for the item using a `NamespacedKey`.
- **`setRecipes(@NotNull List<NamespacedKey> recipes)`** (KnowledgeBookMeta): Clears existing recipes and sets new ones for the Knowledge Book using a list of `NamespacedKey`.
```

### Modern Key Retrieval using RegistryAccess (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/generator/structure/Structure

This example demonstrates the recommended approach for retrieving a structure's key using `RegistryAccess`. It involves obtaining the appropriate registry and then accessing the key for a given `Keyed` object, ensuring compatibility with future API changes.

```java
// Assuming 'keyedObject' is an instance of a Keyed interface (like Structure)
// And 'registryAccess' is an instance of RegistryAccess

Keyed keyedObject = ...; // Your structure instance
RegistryAccess registryAccess = ...; // Get this from the server context

// Get the structure registry
org.bukkit.registry.Registry<Structure> structureRegistry = registryAccess.getRegistry(RegistryKey.STRUCTURE);

// Get the key using the Registry
NamespacedKey structureKey = Registry.getKey(keyedObject);
```

### Plugin Configuration Management (Java)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/Plugin

```Java
FileConfiguration config = plugin.getConfig();

// Save current configuration to file
plugin.saveConfig();

// Reload configuration from file
plugin.reloadConfig();

// Save the default config.yml if it doesn't exist
plugin.saveDefaultConfig();
```

### TextDialogInput Builder

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/io/papermc/paper/registry/data/dialog/input/class-use/TextDialogInput

Explains how to build a TextDialogInput instance using its builder.

```APIDOC
## POST /api/dialog/text/build

### Description
Builds a TextDialogInput object from a builder.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/api/dialog/text/build

### Parameters
#### Request Body
- **builder_config** (object) - Required - Configuration for the TextDialogInput.Builder.
  - **key** (String) - Required - The key for the dialog input.
  - **width** (int) - Required - The width of the dialog input.
  - **label** (Component) - Required - The label to display.
  - **labelVisible** (boolean) - Required - Whether the label is visible.
  - **initial** (String) - Optional - The initial text value.
  - **maxLength** (int) - Optional - The maximum length.
  - **multilineOptions** (MultilineOptions) - Optional - Multiline options.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "builder_config": {
    "key": "message_input",
    "width": 300,
    "label": {"text": "Enter your message"},
    "labelVisible": false,
    "initial": "Hello",
    "maxLength": 512,
    "multilineOptions": {"lines": 5}
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **type** (String) - The type of dialog input.
- **key** (String) - The key of the dialog input.
- **width** (int) - The width of the dialog input.
- **label** (Component) - The label component.
- **labelVisible** (boolean) - Whether the label is visible.
- **initial** (String) - The initial text value.
- **maxLength** (int) - The maximum length.
- **multilineOptions** (MultilineOptions) - The multiline options.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "type": "text",
  "key": "message_input",
  "width": 300,

### PlayerPreLoginEvent Documentation

```APIDOC
## PlayerPreLoginEvent

### Description
This event is fired before a player logs in. It allows plugins to inspect and modify login details, or to deny login. Note that this event is synchronous and the preferred alternative is `AsyncPlayerPreLoginEvent`.

### Method
Event

### Endpoint
N/A (Event-based)

### Parameters
N/A (Event-based)

### Request Example
N/A (Event-based)

### Response
N/A (Event-based)

## Nested Class Summary
### `PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result` (enum)
Deprecated. Basic kick reasons for communicating to plugins.

## Constructor Summary
### `PlayerPreLoginEvent(@NotNull String name, @NotNull InetAddress ipAddress)`
Deprecated, for removal: This API element is subject to removal in a future version.

### `PlayerPreLoginEvent(@NotNull String name, @NotNull InetAddress ipAddress, @NotNull UUID uniqueId)`
Deprecated.

## Method Summary
### `void allow()`
Deprecated. Allows the player to log in.

### `void disallow(@NotNull PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result result, @NotNull String message)`
Deprecated. In favour of `disallow(org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result, Component)`.

### `void disallow(@NotNull PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result result, @NotNull Component message)`
Deprecated. Disallows the player from logging in, with the given reason.

### `@NotNull InetAddress getAddress()`
Deprecated. Gets the player IP address.

### `static @NotNull HandlerList getHandlerList()`
Deprecated.

### `@NotNull HandlerList getHandlers()`
Deprecated.

### `@NotNull String getKickMessage()`
Deprecated. In favour of `kickMessage()`.

### `@NotNull String getName()`
Deprecated. Gets the player's name.

### `@NotNull PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result getResult()`
Deprecated. Gets the current result of the login, as an enum.

### `@NotNull UUID getUniqueId()`
Deprecated. Gets the player's unique ID.

### `@NotNull Component kickMessage()`
Deprecated. Gets the current kick message that will be used when the outcome is not allowed.

### `void kickMessage(@NotNull Component message)`
Deprecated. Sets the kick message to display when the outcome is not allowed.

### `void setKickMessage(@NotNull String message)`
Deprecated. In favour of `kickMessage(Component)`.

### `void setResult(@NotNull PlayerPreLoginEvent.Result result)`
Deprecated. Sets the new result of the login, as an enum.

```APIDOC
## Utility Methods

### `damerauLevenshteinDistance`

#### Description
Computes the Damerau-Levenshtein Distance between two strings. This method is useful for fuzzy string matching.

#### Method
`protected static int damerauLevenshteinDistance(String s1, String s2)`

#### Parameters
- **s1** (String) - The first string being compared.
- **s2** (String) - The second string being compared.

#### Returns
- **distance** (int) - The number of substitutions, deletions, insertions, and transpositions required to transform `s1` into `s2`.

### `findPossibleMatches`

#### Description
Finds possible help topics that match a given search string.

#### Method
`protected HelpTopic findPossibleMatches(String searchString)`

#### Parameters
- **searchString** (String) - The string to search for.

#### Returns
- **topic** (HelpTopic) - The matching HelpTopic, or null if no matches are found.
```

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/plugin/PluginLoader

Methods for retrieving plugin descriptions and creating registered listeners.

```APIDOC
## POST /getPluginDescription

### Description
Loads a PluginDescriptionFile from the specified file.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/getPluginDescription

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **file** (File) - Required - The file to attempt to load from.

### Request Example
{
  "file": "path/to/plugin.yml"
}

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **pluginDescriptionFile** (PluginDescriptionFile) - A new PluginDescriptionFile loaded from the plugin.yml in the specified file.

#### Response Example
{
  "pluginDescriptionFile": {
    "name": "MyPlugin",
    "version": "1.0"
  }
}

### Errors
- **InvalidDescriptionException**: If the plugin description file could not be created.
```

```APIDOC
## POST /createRegisteredListeners

### Description
Creates and returns registered listeners for the event classes used in this listener.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/createRegisteredListeners

### Parameters
#### Path Parameters
None

#### Query Parameters
None

#### Request Body
- **listener** (Listener) - Required - The object that will handle the eventual call back.
- **plugin** (Plugin) - Required - The plugin to use when creating registered listeners.

### Request Example
{
  "listener": "com.example.MyListener",
  "plugin": "com.example.MyPlugin"
}

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **registeredListeners** (Map<Class<? extends Event>,Set<RegisteredListener>>) - The registered listeners.

#### Response Example
{
  "registeredListeners": {
    "org.bukkit.event.player.PlayerJoinEvent": [
      "com.example.MyListener#onPlayerJoin"
    ]
  }
}

### PlayerFishEvent.State API Documentation

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/event/player/class-use/PlayerFishEvent.State

Provides details on the PlayerFishEvent.State enum, including methods to get the state, convert from String, and get all states. Also includes PlayerFishEvent constructors that utilize this state.

```APIDOC
## PlayerFishEvent.State Enum Documentation

### Description
Represents the possible states of a fishing event.

### Methods
#### `getState()`
- **Description**: Gets the current state of the fishing event.
- **Returns**: `@NotNull PlayerFishEvent.State`

#### `valueOf(String name)`
- **Description**: Returns the enum constant of this class with the specified name.
- **Parameters**:
  - **name** (String) - The name of the enum constant to return.
- **Returns**: `static PlayerFishEvent.State`

#### `values()`
- **Description**: Returns an array containing the constants of this enum class, in the order they are declared.
- **Returns**: `static PlayerFishEvent.State[]`

### Constructors for PlayerFishEvent
#### `PlayerFishEvent(@NotNull Player player, @Nullable Entity entity, @NotNull FishHook hookEntity, @NotNull PlayerFishEvent.State state)`
- **Description**: Constructs a new PlayerFishEvent with the given player, entity, fish hook, and fishing state.
- **Parameters**:
  - **player** (`@NotNull Player`) - The player performing the fishing action.
  - **entity** (`@Nullable Entity`) - The entity that was caught (can be null).
  - **hookEntity** (`@NotNull FishHook`) - The fish hook entity.
  - **state** (`@NotNull PlayerFishEvent.State`) - The current state of the fishing event.

#### `PlayerFishEvent(@NotNull Player player, @Nullable Entity entity, @NotNull FishHook hookEntity, @Nullable EquipmentSlot hand, @NotNull PlayerFishEvent.State state)`
- **Description**: Constructs a new PlayerFishEvent with the given player, entity, fish hook, equipment slot, and fishing state.
- **Parameters**:
  - **player** (`@NotNull Player`) - The player performing the fishing action.
  - **entity** (`@Nullable Entity`) - The entity that was caught (can be null).
  - **hookEntity** (`@NotNull FishHook`) - The fish hook entity.
  - **hand** (`@Nullable EquipmentSlot`) - The equipment slot used for fishing.
  - **state** (`@NotNull PlayerFishEvent.State`) - The current state of the fishing event.
```

### Set Resource Pack (with Prompt)

Source: https://jd.papermc.io/paper/1.21.11/org/bukkit/entity/Player

Requests the player's client to download and switch to a specified resource pack, including a custom prompt to be displayed to the player. This enhances user experience by providing context.

```APIDOC
## POST /setResourcePack

### Description
Requests the player's client to download and switch to a specified resource pack, including a custom prompt to be displayed to the player. This enhances user experience by providing context.

### Method
POST

### Endpoint
/setResourcePack

### Parameters
#### Query Parameters
- **url** (string) - Required - The URL from which the client will download the resource pack. Must contain only US-ASCII characters and be encoded as per RFC 1738.
- **hash** (string) - Required - A 40 character hexadecimal and lowercase SHA-1 digest of the resource pack file.
- **required** (boolean) - Required - Marks if the resource pack should be required by the client.
- **resourcePackPrompt** (Component) - Optional - A Component representing the prompt to be displayed to the client.

### Request Example
```json
{
  "url": "http://example.com/resourcepack.zip",
  "hash": "a1b2c3d4e5f678901234567890abcdef12345678",
  "required": true,
  "resourcePackPrompt": {
    "text": "This server uses a custom resource pack. Do you want to download it?"
  }
}
```

### Response
#### Success Response (200)
- **message** (string) - Indicates successful request.

#### Response Example
```json
{
  "message": "Resource pack request sent successfully."
}
```

#### Error Handling
- **IllegalArgumentException** - Thrown if the URL is null or too long.
```

--------------------------------
